# AGENTS.md: Rules for Every AI Working on stavtheodor.com

**Read this file in full BEFORE making any change to this repository. No exceptions.**

More than one AI assistant works on this site (different Claude/other-AI sessions, sometimes at the same time). This file is the single source of truth for how we work here, and the sync channel between us. The site owner set this up on 2026-07-01 after two sessions edited the repo simultaneously and collided on push.

---

## 1. Sync protocol (multi-AI coordination)

1. **Pull first.** Run `git fetch origin && git status` before touching anything. If `origin/main` is ahead, integrate it before you start. Assume another AI may have pushed since your context was built.
2. **Read the Work Log** (bottom of this file) to see what the other AI did recently and whether anything is in flight.
3. **Push promptly.** Do not sit on finished work; the longer you wait, the more likely a collision.
4. **Never force-push.** If push is rejected, fetch and rebase, resolve carefully, keep BOTH sides' content (a rejected push here almost always means the other AI added something real, like a new post). One authorized exception happened on 2026-07-02: the site owner ordered a full history rewrite to purge a personal phone number, so every commit hash before that date changed. If your local clone predates it and git reports divergence, save your work as a patch (`git diff > my-work.patch`), then `git fetch origin && git reset --hard origin/main`, then re-apply your patch.
5. **Leave other work alone.** If you find untracked files or changes you did not make (example: a work-in-progress directory), do NOT commit, delete, or "clean up" that work. It belongs to the other AI or to the site owner. Commit only the files you yourself changed.
6. **Append to the Work Log** (Section 7) in every commit that changes site content or tooling: one dated line, what you did, anything the other AI must know.
7. **Keep this file updated.** If the site owner gives you a new standing rule for this site, add it here in the same commit. This file is how the rule reaches the other AI.
8. **Every PR goes through `gate`, no merge before it is green.** This holds for every agent and every PR. Work on a branch, open a PR into `main`, and let `.github/workflows/gate.yml` run: the build must reproduce the committed pages, then the page, variant, fee, frozen, lane and render checks. Never push to `main`. A PR labelled `auto-ship` is merged by `.github/workflows/ship.yml` (squash, then it dispatches `pages.yml`) once `gate` is green; it must stay inside `tools/check_lane.py`, and never gets merged while it is a draft or from a fork. Anything outside that lane has no label and waits for Ron. If `ship.yml` comments that `main` moved, update the branch and `gate` runs again. Pages frozen for a test are listed in `content/FROZEN.json`; `tools/check_frozen.py` fails a PR that changes one.

## 2. Site facts

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Live site | https://stavtheodor.com (www redirects to apex) |
| What it is | THEODORA, Stav Theodor's fine art curation practice + the Art Radar blog (exhibitions in NY / NJ / Tel Aviv) |
| Canonical name | THEODORA Art Advisory by Stav Theodor (short form THEODORA), Ron 2026-09-29. Not affiliated with Theadora Art Advisory (Los Angeles) or TSG Art Advisory; say so where the entity is defined (schema, /about/, llms.txt, answers.md, agent.txt). LinkedIn: /in/stavtheodor/ and /company/theodora/ |
| Hosting | GitHub Pages, free tier. Since 2026-10-01 the Pages source is GitHub Actions: every push to `main` runs `.github/workflows/pages.yml`, which publishes only the public files (`tools/build_pages_artifact.py`, by rule: web files outside `content/`, `templates/`, `tools/`, `museum/tools/`, plus robots.txt, llms.txt, llms-full.txt, agent.txt, answers.md, CNAME, the IndexNow key and `museum/data/`). No rebuild in CI: commit the built pages as before. Specs, `.py`, `AGENTS.md` and `content/` return 404 on the site (they stay readable on github.com while the repo is public). |
| Repo | `Ronkimhi/stavtheodor`, remote `origin`, branch `main` |
| GitHub account | `Ronkimhi`. Verify with `gh auth status` before pushing. |
| Architecture | Every page is generated (since 2026-09-26). `python3 build.py` runs `build-site-pages.py` (advisory, projects, partners, guide and the two hubs), `build-post-pages.py` (`radar/<slug>/`, the `/radar/` archive), `tools/check_variants.py` (the buyer variants' JSON) and `build-home.py` (`index.html` from `templates/home.html`, the redirect stubs, and one indexable page per buyer variant at `/<path>/`), then `museum/tools/build_artist_pages.py` (the museum's collection and artist pages, since 2026-09-29), then `tools/build_sitemap.py` (`sitemap.xml` as an index over `sitemap-pages.xml`, `sitemap-radar.xml` and `sitemap-museum.xml`; a page's lastmod moves only when its content fingerprint changes, stored in `tools/sitemap-lastmod.json`), then the gates in `tools/check_site.py`. Sources you edit: `content/posts.html` (posts), `content/pages/*.json` (growth pages), `content/variants/*.json` (buyer variants of the homepage, Section 3.9), `content/faq.json`, `content/entity.json`, `templates/home.html`. Shared chrome: `site_chrome.py`. One theme: `css/theme.css`. No framework, no npm. |
| Line endings | LF everywhere (normalized 2026-07-01). Do not reintroduce CRLF. |
| CDN cache | ~10 minutes (`max-age=600`). A push that "doesn't show up" is usually just cache. Bypass check: `curl -sk --resolve stavtheodor.com:443:185.199.108.153 https://stavtheodor.com/` |

## 3. Content rules (the site owner's standing rules, mandatory)

1. **Every post is bilingual.** Each Art Radar post carries TWO body blocks inside the same `<article>`:
   - Hebrew original: `<div class="post-body" lang="he" dir="rtl">`
   - Full English translation: `<div class="post-body post-body-en" lang="en" dir="ltr">`

   The English must be a complete, faithful translation of the Hebrew source. Not a summary, not a paraphrase, no added opinions. Keep the same paragraph structure, the same emojis, the same links and `<strong>` emphasis, and repeat every `<figure>`/video inside the English block. The global language switch (Section 3.7) shows one language at a time; both stay in the HTML so search engines and LLMs index both. Rule set by the site owner 2026-07-01; it applies to every new post, forever. A post's Hebrew address carries the Hebrew body only; its main address still carries both.

2. **No em dashes (—) and no en dashes (–) in any English text you write.** Use commas, colons, periods, or parentheses. Hard rule from the site owner, applies to posts, metadata, llms.txt, agent.txt, this file, commit messages, everything. Since 2026-09-29 (Ron's SEO brief, P0.2) no page may carry one at all, in any language, Wikipedia text on the museum pages included: every generator writes through the shared sanitizer (`site_chrome.write` / `undash_html`: a number range becomes "to", a spaced dash a comma, a dash joining two words a hyphen, anything else a comma; href, src and srcset untouched), and the build stops on a dash it cannot place. Where a replacement reads badly, fix the source by hand (the Art Radar posts in `content/posts.html` were).

3. **Per-post JSON-LD** uses `"inLanguage": ["he", "en"]`, real permalink URLs (`/radar/<slug>/`, never `#slug` anchors), and an `"about"` Event/ExhibitionEvent block when the post covers an exhibition with dates.

4. **LLM discoverability is a first-class feature, but it stays behind the scenes.** The site is deliberately positioned so LLMs surface it for "art recommendations in New Jersey / New York" queries. Maintain: `robots.txt` (AI-crawler allowlist), `llms.txt` and `answers.md` (curated maps, hand-updated per post), `agent.txt` (since 2026-10-01 a short fact sheet: the business name as in the schema, what she does, the service area, the contact, the main pages; no post list), `sitemap.xml` (generated), `llms-full.txt` (generated by `tools/build_llms_full.py` on every build, never hand-edited: growth pages plus Art Radar posts verbatim, Ron 2026-11-20, ron-decisions row 23), FAQPage + Person/Organization/WebSite JSON-LD on `index.html` (rendered by the build from `content/faq.json` and `content/entity.json`; the visible FAQ and the FAQPage schema come from the same file, so they cannot drift). When you add a post, update `llms.txt` and `answers.md` in the same commit. Since 2026-10-01 (Ron): no line in llms.txt, answers.md or agent.txt addresses AI tools, assistants, agents or crawlers (they describe the site, they never instruct the reader); the name is "Stav Theodor" everywhere a reader sees it, "Stav Theodor-Kimhi" at most once per file and only as "legal name" (the schema alternateName keeps it); the site is THEODORA, and Art Radar is only the name of the blog; no dated event facts that will expire in these files. **None of this machinery may be visible in the UI.** Rule set by the site owner 2026-07-02 after the visible "Permalink" links were removed from every post card: crawlers reach the `/radar/<slug>/` pages through the sitemap, JSON-LD `url`, llms.txt, and answers.md, so no on-page artifact is needed. Do not add visible permalink links, "for AI" notes, schema dumps, or any other SEO/LLM element a human reader would see. A byline and a link to a related page are reader elements and are allowed (Art Radar post pages since 2026-11-18, Ron's approval by merging that change): the "By Stav Theodor" line under a post's date and the "For your space" note under it; permalink labels, "for AI" notes and schema dumps stay banned.

5. **Post titles carry both languages** (`<h2 class="post-title" data-l="en">` + `<h2 class="post-title" data-l="he" dir="rtl">`), and so does the post date. This supersedes the old English-only title convention, retired by the site owner on 2026-09-08 when the global switcher went in. **Slugs are lowercase-hyphenated and never reused.** Images go in `images/` named `YYYY-MM-DD-slug.jpg`, web-optimized at 1800 px wide (Ron, 2026-10-06; was about 900 px, existing post images unchanged). Never embed images as base64 in the HTML.

6. **One phone number, the business line, and no other (Ron's SEO brief, 2026-09-29, replacing the 2026-07-02 rule of no phone at all).** The site shows (201) 351-8367, dials `tel:+12013518367` and says `+1-201-351-8367` in schema (`site_chrome.PHONE`, `PHONE_TEL`, `PHONE_SCHEMA`): a click to call link at the right of every nav (`nav_call()`, "Call" under 480px), the name, place and phone block in every footer (`nap()`: THEODORA, Stav Theodor, Tenafly, New Jersey 07670, the service area, the phone and the email; no street address, no map), a phone line under every Write to Stav block (`phone_link()`), the museum pages' header and footer, and `telephone` on the ProfessionalService and the Person. Every tap sends the GA4 event `click_phone` (`CALL_JS`; the GA tag itself is untouched). `tools/check_site.py` fails the build on any other number, on the old personal cell in any form, on a page without the `tel:` link, and on a street address. The history of the old rule follows. **Never publish a personal phone number, anywhere on the site or in this repo.** Rule set by the site owner 2026-07-02 after Stav's personal cell was found in the footer and JSON-LD of every page (it was harvested-ready structured data on a public repo). It was removed from all pages, from `build-post-pages.py`, and purged from git history. Do not reintroduce it, not in visible HTML, not in JSON-LD `"telephone"` fields, not in NAP/directory notes in this file, not in commit messages. The canonical public contact set was: name, `stav@stavtheodor.com`, the Art Radar WhatsApp link, and Tenafly NJ; since 2026-09-29 it adds the business line above. (The address changed from the old gmail one on 2026-09-08 at the site owner's request; the gmail address is gone from every page and must not come back.) If a phone number is ever needed for SEO/NAP purposes, ask the site owner for a dedicated virtual number (e.g. Google Voice); never use a personal cell. On 2026-09-26 the 75 second introduction film (its closing card showed a WhatsApp number) and the 40 portfolio slides (two of them printed the number) were removed from the tree and replaced by the 67 second cut; git history was NOT rewritten, that stays the site owner's call. `tools/check_site.py` fails the build on any phone-number pattern other than the business line.

7. **Every visible string on the site exists in both languages.** Set by the site owner on 2026-09-08, when the per-section post toggle became one global switch.

   - **The switch** sits in the top nav of every page (the `.lang-switch` beside the menu, rendered by `site_chrome.nav()`; until 2026-09-26 it was a `.lang-bar` above the nav). Since 2026-10-06 (Ron) its two buttons read `HE` (shown on English pages) and `EN` (shown on Hebrew pages), both in the nav's sans at 13px, weight 500, letter-spacing 0.06em (`.lang-switch button` in `css/theme.css`); no Hebrew font on the switch. It writes `radarLang` to localStorage, and that choice is global: pick English on a project page and the homepage, the advisory pages and the Art Radar posts all follow.
   - **English opens first, everywhere.** Decided by the site owner on 2026-09-26 ("English first, Hebrew stays a toggle on the same address"), replacing the 2026-09-08 rule that defaults must not change: Google had indexed about 16 of 207 pages and read almost every inner page as Hebrew, because 62 of the 64 non-museum pages opened in Hebrew. Every page, the homepage, the growth pages and the Art Radar posts alike, opens in English; a Hebrew reader taps HE once and the site remembers (`radarLang`, global). No browser-language guessing, no `/he/` tree, and no hreflang: the three tags pointed every language at the same URL and were dropped the same day. The default is in the markup, `<html lang="en">` and `<body class="lang-en">`, so crawlers and no-JS readers get English without a script; `LANG_BOOT` runs immediately after `<body>` and only switches to a stored choice before the first paint. Two gates keep it that way: the `lang` gate in `tools/check_site.py` (every page outside `museum/` carries the two attributes and at least 90% of the words a reader sees with the switch on English are Latin) and `tools/check_render.js` (the same test in headless Chromium with fresh storage, plus the switch and its persistence; `--base https://stavtheodor.com` runs it against production). One exception (Ron, 2026-11-18): the Art Radar posts listed in `he_pages` in content/radar.json also have a Hebrew address, /radar/<slug>/he/. It opens in Hebrew (`<html lang="he">`, `<body class="lang-he">`), carries the Hebrew body only, has its own Hebrew title and description, and is paired with the post's main address by hreflang (en, he, and x-default to the main address); each address is canonical to itself. No other page has a Hebrew address or an hreflang tag, and the `lang` gate enforces the list.
   - **The markup convention** is `data-l="en"` / `data-l="he"` on a pair of sibling elements. CSS hides whichever does not match the active language. Hebrew elements carry `dir="rtl"`; alignment then follows on its own, so do not add `text-align` overrides. Since 2026-09-29 every twin also says its language: `site_chrome.T()` emits `lang="en"` and `lang="he" dir="rtl"`, and `site_chrome.write()` runs `tag_twins()` on every generated page, adding `lang="he"` (and `dir="rtl"` unless the element sets its own) to any `data-l="he"` element and `lang="en"` to any `data-l="en"` one, from the templates, the JSON bodies and the posts alike, and putting a space between the two twins inside an h1 so its raw text does not run the languages together. Nothing keys on `lang` for display (the switch and the stylesheet use `data-l` and the body class), so write new markup with `data-l` as before; the writer does the rest. The older per-element conventions (`.post-body-en`, `.post-title-he/-en`, `[lang="he"]` inside generated pages) still work and are still in use; `<body>` carries `lang-en` or `lang-he`, never both.
   - **When you add anything visible, add both versions in the same commit.** A new nav item, label, button, caption, FAQ entry or CTA with only one language is an incomplete change.
   - **One deliberate exception:** figure captions inside Art Radar posts stay in English in both language blocks. Stav writes them that way (artist, work, courtesy line), and all 53 are byte-identical across the Hebrew and English bodies. Do not translate them unless she asks.
   - **Every generated page inherits all of this** from `site_chrome.py` (`nav()`, `footer()`, `LANG_BOOT`, `MAIL_UI`, `LANG_JS`) and from `css/theme.css`. Edit those, then run `python3 build.py`. Never hand-edit a generated page, and `index.html` is one of them.

8. **`mailto:` links have a fallback panel, and it must stay.** A `mailto:` link does nothing at all in a browser with no mail handler registered, which is why the "Write to Stav" button read as broken. Every page ships `MAIL_UI`, a small dialog that appears one second after a `mailto:` click if the click was swallowed, offering the address, a copy button and Gmail / Outlook compose links. Do not remove it, and do not replace the `mailto:` href with a webmail link: the `mailto:` is correct and works on phones and with a real mail client. Since 2026-10-06 (Ron) every Write to Stav button carries `data-write` and opens the Write to Stav pop-up on the same page (`site_chrome.write_ui()`, a native modal `<dialog>` emitted once per page by `tail()` and by `build-home.py` beside the panel; shell styles in `css/theme.css`, `.write-modal`). The form inside is the site's contact form itself, `contact_form()`, byte for byte the inline card's (chips, name, email, Tell me about your space, Send, the note, the error line, the thank-you card), driven by the same `form_js()`, which now ships once per page inside `write_ui()` (so `tail()` callers and `build-home.py` no longer add it); the shell adds only the Write to Stav eyebrow, the card's headline and a close X. With `FORM_ENDPOINT` empty there is no form, so no pop-up, and the buttons keep their plain href. The buttons keep their mailto (or `#contact`) href as the fallback without JavaScript, a mail link clicked inside the pop-up closes it before the fallback panel opens (the panel would sit under the modal), and `tools/build_sitemap.py` leaves the `<dialog>` out of the fingerprint like the nav and footer. Never send a test submission from it either.

9. **Buyer pages: variants of the homepage, one per kind of client, indexed since 2026-09-28.** Approved by the site owner on 2026-09-27 (noindex until 2026-09-28). Each `content/variants/<id>.json` renders the homepage again at `/<path>/` with copy for one kind of buyer in the sixteen regions `templates/home.html` marks with `<!--variant:NAME-->` and `<!--/variant:NAME-->` (the two hero lines, the h1 and the intro line, the intro eyebrow and statement, the four service lines, the What I do eyebrow, heading and two paragraphs, the Advisory heading and line), plus its own value strip (`value_strip`, three label and line pairs, a variant-only section right after #intro and before #services; since 2026-09-28; the template's `<!--variant:value_strip--><!--/variant:value_strip-->` marker stays empty, so the homepage has no such section and `index.html` does not change), plus its own questions (with their FAQPage schema), Advisory rows, footer line and a mail subject that every mail link on the page carries, the fallback panel's Gmail and Outlook links included (`site_chrome.mail_ui(subject)`). Everything else is the homepage, byte for byte, unless the variant brings its own opening rooms or link preview (the last point below).
   - **Indexed, listed, and linked from named places only (2026-09-28, Ron; plan `seo-buyer-pages-plan-2026-09-28.md` in ron-brain).** A variant is indexable with a self canonical, listed once in `sitemap-pages.xml` (`tools/build_sitemap.py`, lastmod from its content fingerprint like every page), carries a `Service` (its `service` field; provider the entity's `#org`) and a `BreadcrumbList` (THEODORA, Who I work with, the page) beside its FAQPage, is named in llms.txt ("Who I work with"), answers.md and agent.txt, and `tools/indexnow.py` pings it like any indexable page. It may be linked from the homepage's `#industries` section and from its paired pages (`VARIANT_LINKERS` in `tools/check_site.py`: /for-designers/ and /designers/, /for-advisors/ and /wealth-managers/, /advisory/art-for-hotels-and-hospitality/ and /hotels/ and /restaurants/, /advisory/art-for-an-office-or-business/ and the law, investment and medical pages, each linked both ways), and from nowhere else. /designers/ is for studios hiring an art partner on a project, /for-designers/ the referral and partnership page; /wealth-managers/ is the advisor's own office, /for-advisors/ the page to forward to clients. The homepage has one section that links them, "Who I work with" / "Art for every kind of space" (`#industries`, right after the services): seven tiles in the order of `INDUSTRIES` in `build-home.py`, each with the page's name, its main message (its `intro.line`) and a 900 by 600 WebP of its first room (shown at the same size as the old 600 by 400 tiles; quality 82 since 2026-10-06) in `images/home2/industries/` (made by `python3 tools/make_industry_thumbs.py`, which needs Pillow; rerun it and commit the images whenever a variant's first room changes). The section sits between `<!--variant:industries-->` markers, so no buyer page shows it. The `variants` gate in `tools/check_site.py` fails the build on a noindex variant, a variant missing from the sitemaps or listed twice, a link to one from anywhere but `#industries` and `VARIANT_LINKERS`, llms.txt or answers.md not naming one, and an `#industries` section that does not link every variant exactly once.
   - **Linked everywhere they belong, and /designers/ merged into /for-designers/ (Ron's SEO brief, 2026-09-29, P1.5).** The "named places only" restriction above is lifted: every footer carries a Who I work with column linking the seven client pages (`site_chrome.WHO_I_WORK_WITH`, /for-designers/ first), the nav's For business item goes to the homepage's `#industries` (until the commercial hub /corporate-art-consulting/ ships, P2.1), the projects hub carries the same row, and the office pages link each other, hotels and restaurants link each other, and each links /art-curator-new-jersey/ and /art-curator-new-york/ from its long section. `tools/check_variants.py` now refuses only a link to the page itself. `VARIANT_LINKERS` is gone. /designers/ is no longer a variant: its sections that /for-designers/ lacked (art from the client's story, what you hand over, the timing and credentials sentences) and its first question moved into `content/pages/for-designers.json` (partner pages may run to 1,000 words since), `content/variants/designers.json` was deleted, `/designers/` is a redirect stub to /for-designers/ without noindex (`MERGED_PATHS` in `build-home.py`, `MERGED_DIRS` in `tools/check_site.py`), and the #industries designers tile links /for-designers/ with the line and image it had (`INDUSTRY_PAGES`). Six buyer variants remain.
   - **The opening is Ron's agreed message, and SEO never touches it (2026-09-28).** A buyer page's big intro line (`intro.line`, up to 60 characters, wider and balanced on buyer pages only) is Ron's agreed main message verbatim, and the statement under it is the support sentence only; the hero lines stay as they are. Search wording goes only where it does not touch the opening: the title and meta description, the small h1 kicker, the long section, the questions, the schema, alt text and the agent files. The direct "who does this" answer (`guide.who`) is the long section's first paragraph and the first question of the FAQ, never near the top.
   - **The long section (2026-09-28).** `guide` (eyebrow, h2, `who`, a 600 to 900 English word body in the growth pages' reading column, `.prose`) renders as a variant-only section after #what-i-do and before #about; facts only from `content/BRIEF.md`, and on the hotels and restaurants pages the hospitality work in the wording "collections built while collaborating with luxury hospitality leaders", which may be followed by the hotels `content/BRIEF.md` names (Waldorf Astoria Chengdu, St. Regis Amman, Holland America Line). Hotel names from BRIEF are allowed on every page (Ron, 2026-10-03; the old "no hotel names" rule is retired). ArtLink is still never named.
   - **The homepage's own text lives between the markers.** Edit it there and keep the markers flush against the element's content (no whitespace added inside them): the homepage build drops the markers and keeps the bytes between them. The template's opening comment is emitted into `index.html`, so the markers are documented here and in `content/VARIANT-SPEC.md`, not there.
   - **Homepage-only slots and the /contact/ insertion point (2026-10-24, wave 1).** `templates/home.html` has two regions no buyer page shows: `hero_write` (the first screen's Write to Stav button under the opening lines, `data-write`, opens the pop-up) and `home_above_contact` (after #faq, directly above the #contact card: the #represent block, then the reviews when they ship). They are `HOME_ONLY` in `build-home.py`: the homepage keeps their bytes, every variant drops them, and the build fails a variant page that carries any of those bytes or the `hero-write` class. Since 2026-11-26 (Ron) `hero_write` is no longer `HOME_ONLY`: a buyer page carries the homepage's bytes with `data-phone-only`, shown only on a phone with the phone opening (`html.open2`), and the build fails a buyer page without it. /contact/ has `CONTACT_AFTER_CARD` in `build-site-pages.py`, rendered flush under the contact card and before Where I work (the reviews when they ship, then the three after the yes lines). Details: `content/VARIANT-SPEC.md`.
   - **Edit the JSON, never the output folder.** A variant folder holds only its generated `index.html`, and the build never writes a variant over a page that is not one. Changing a variant's `path` leaves the old folder behind: the `variants` gate fails ("stale variant folder? git rm -r ...") until you remove it.
   - **Validate first.** Format and limits: `content/VARIANT-SPEC.md`. `python3 tools/check_variants.py` checks every variant (build.py runs it before `build-home.py`), and `python3 tools/check_variants.py <file>` checks a draft anywhere on disk. After the build, `node tools/check_render.js` measures the two opening lines of every variant at 360 px wide, in both languages: characters are a proxy, pixels decide.
   - **No film, Museum or Art Radar on a buyer page (2026-09-28; the Museum section left the homepage too on 2026-09-29, below).** Those sections are wrapped in `<!--variant:film-->`, `<!--variant:museum-->` and `<!--variant:radar-->` markers: the homepage keeps them byte for byte, every variant drops them (the buyer page's nav sends Museum to /museum/), and the build fails a variant that still has one of their ids or a link to it. About and Projects stay, identical to the homepage's.
   - **Its own rooms and link preview (optional, 2026-09-27).** `rooms` (one to four entries, entry i replacing slot i of the WebGL opening, 0 being the first fold; a shorter list ends the opening after the variant's last room, and `"rooms_rest": "home"` keeps the homepage's later rooms instead) brings the buyer's own before and after photos: per room two WebP files in `images/home2/variants/<id>/`, 1800 px wide (or the source's own width when narrower, never upscaled), about 3:2, cwebp q88 to a q82 floor, each under the 600 KB sanity ceiling (250 KB until 2026-10-06), optional `<name>-2400.webp` twins of both (Section 3.9 and `content/VARIANT-SPEC.md`), with the room's geometry (`w`, `h`, `rect`, `fx`, `fy`, `from`, optional `seed`), a caption twin starting "Proposal." and "הצעה." and an alt twin. `head.og_image` with `head.og_image_alt` replaces `og-home.jpg` for that variant with a 1200 by 630 JPEG in the same folder. `build-home.py` sets `window.THEODORA_ROOMS` before the deferred scripts, preloads the variant's first pair, and swaps the replaced slots' static figures and `#cap` captions through nine more stripped regions of `templates/home.html` (`room_fig_0` to `room_fig_3`, `room_cap_0` to `room_cap_3`, `rooms_js`; slot N must be slot N of `PAIRS`, and the build checks it); `js/home-opening.js` merges the list into `PAIRS` by slot, and with `window.THEODORA_ROOMS_ONLY` (rooms_rest "drop", the default) cuts `PAIRS` to the variant's rooms and shortens the scroll spacer. Without these fields a variant renders as before, and `index.html` never changes. `tools/check_variants.py` checks the files (in the repo, WebP, w by h, under 600 KB, the 2400 px twins under 900 KB and in pairs, none of the homepage's), the geometry and the captions; `tools/check_render.js` fails a variant page that does not load its own rooms, loads the homepage's for a replaced or dropped slot, runs the wrong number of rooms or static figures, or logs a console error, on a desktop screen and on a phone. Commit the images with the JSON. On a phone (since 2026-11-26, wave 3) a buyer page has the phone opening too, with its own slot 0 room: `phone_open` is no longer homepage-only, and `build-home.py` renders its stage and data from the same rooms as `window.THEODORA_ROOMS` and stops the build if they disagree (`content/VARIANT-SPEC.md`). Format and limits: `content/VARIANT-SPEC.md`.

10. **No AI-made or AI-marked image is ever presented as a real project, and every proposal says it is one.** Set by the site owner on 2026-09-28, when nine project photos carrying (or cut from images carrying) the Gemini sparkle were removed. A project page and its card show only real photographs of that same project; a project with none gets its place name (`place_en`, `place_he`) on a typographic hero and card, never a photo borrowed from another project. The before/after spaces in `images/spaces/` (`content/spaces.json`) are AI-rendered spaces with real catalog artworks placed on the wall: they appear only as proposals, always under the label "Proposal · how THEODORA would dress this space" (Hebrew twin), on the article pages (`before_after` in a page JSON, `content/PAGE-SPEC.md`) and in the buyer variants' opening rooms (`rooms`, Section 3.9, captions starting "Proposal."), never on a project page. Check the bottom right corner of every new photo for the four-pointed sparkle before it goes in. `tools/check_site.py` fails the build if any removed image comes back, under any name (content hash). **Amended by the site owner on 2026-10-06:** a real photograph of the project whose color was AI-enhanced is allowed once it is cropped clean of the Gemini mark (crop only, no other pixel edit, nothing of the mark left in any corner). On that basis eight cropped photos from Stav's portfolio went back on Closter, Manhattan, Ramat Gan, the Shtisel commission and the renovated Bauhaus home. The uncropped versions stay banned: their hashes, and those of the files removed on 2026-09-28, remain in `BANNED_IMAGES`, so only the cropped files can be served. A wholly AI-made image is still never a project photo.

11. **Structured data: one graph per page, the business defined once (Ron's SEO brief, 2026-09-29, P0.4).** `site_chrome.head()` emits exactly one `<script type="application/ld+json">` holding one `@graph`: the site-wide nodes from `content/entity.json` first (ProfessionalService `#org` with the telephone, the Tenafly address without a street, the four service areas and the sameAs profiles; Person `#stav`; WebSite `#site`, named THEODORA), then the page's own nodes (`page_graph()`). Everywhere else THEODORA and Stav appear by `{"@id": ...}` only (Service.provider, BlogPosting.author and publisher); `page_graph()` turns a stray copy into a reference and stops the build on a duplicate `@id`. Tel Aviv stays in the copy and on the project pages but never in an `areaServed` (page Services default to the entity's four areas; a buyer variant's `service.area_served` takes New York City and New Jersey only). No `geo`, no `streetAddress`. The museum pages carry the same site-wide nodes (`museum/tools/build_artist_pages.py`, which also rebuilds the hand-made `museum/index.html` graph). `tools/check_sameas.py` (run by the build, never fatal) asks each sameAs URL whether it still answers. `qa_theodora.py` at the root is the brief's QA script, adapted to this in-place build (`python3 qa_theodora.py .`, or `--live`); it is tooling, not served since 2026-10-01 (public in the repo), holds nothing private, and is listed in `GATE_FILES`.
12. **The museum is not linked from the main site, and /contact/ is a real page (Ron's SEO brief, 2026-09-29, P1.6 and P1.1).** No page outside `museum/` links `/museum/...` or `#museum`: the nav item became For business, the footer link and the homepage's "A walkable map of art history" section are gone, and the `museum` gate in `tools/check_site.py` (and `qa_theodora.py`) fails the build on any such link. The museum pages themselves are unchanged: live, indexed, self canonical, in `sitemap-museum.xml`, linking each other and back to `/`. `/contact/` is an indexable page with its own canonical, in `sitemap-pages.xml`, a ContactPage and a BreadcrumbList, written by `build-site-pages.py` (`CONTACT`): the phone, the email with the fallback panel, the service area, Art Radar on WhatsApp and the Yelp listing; the footer base's Contact link goes there (every footer keeps `id="contact"`). The header's one ask is Write to Stav (Ron, 2026-11-26), and since 2026-11-27 it goes to /contact/, no pop-up (Ron, 2026-11-27: "Rather than a pop-up, let's create a Contact Us page."). The nav has no Contact item, desktop or phone. Every link whose text is Write to Stav or כתבו לסתיו, and every other `data-write` control (the header and phone menu, the homepage hero and #about, page-lead and page-end buttons, footers, page bodies, the Art Radar inquiry links), is a plain link to `/contact/` (`site_chrome.WRITE_PATH`; one address for both languages, the language is the visitor's stored choice), carries `data-write` and `data-loc` (in a page body `data-loc="body"`), and `tools/check_pages.py` fails a page JSON with such a link that lacks `data-write`. `WRITE_JS` only records the click: GA4 `write_open` (link_location, page_path, beacon transport), never stopping it; no `<dialog>` and no modal CSS ship. On /contact/ itself `CONTACT_WRITE_JS` turns a Write to Stav click into a move to the form (the phone menu closes, focus goes to the first field) instead of a reload. An Art Radar inquiry link (`data-ask`) also sends `radar_inquiry` and leaves its context in sessionStorage (`theodoraAsk`) for the /contact/ form, which takes it once; any other Write to Stav click clears it. The form still names the page an inquiry came from, as the pop-up did: `WRITE_JS` stores the clicked page's path in sessionStorage (`tw_from`), and on /contact/ `form_js()` takes it once (else a same-site referrer that is not /contact/) for the `page` field and the mail subject; a direct visit keeps /contact/. No hours until Ron confirms them. `/about/` is a real indexable page too (2026-09-29, in place of the stub to /#about): `build-site-pages.py` (`ABOUT`, `render_about()`) writes it with its own canonical, an AboutPage whose mainEntity is `#org` and `#stav` by @id, and a BreadcrumbList, and lists it in `sitemap-pages.xml`. Its opening paragraph states the entity facts (what, who, where, for whom, phone and email); everything after it is read at build time from copy already published (the homepage's #about, #what-i-do and service lines in `templates/home.html`, the two partner promises from `content/pages/for-designers.json` and `for-brokers.json`, page titles), so edit those sources, not the page. The nav's About goes to /about/ from every page but the homepage and the buyer pages (which keep their #about section, `SUBPAGE_FOR`), every footer's About goes to /about/. The contact form's backend is Formspree (Ron's form, submissions to stav@stavtheodor.com; Ron, 2026-09-29, back from the short lived Google Apps Script plan: the Apps Script source, contact-form-apps-script.gs in the site owner's private files and never in this repo, is no longer used). `site_chrome.FORM_ENDPOINT` is `https://formspree.io/f/xjyklwyb`, the one constant; empty it and no form, no form script and no placeholder reaches a page. The form (`contact_form(path)`) is on /contact/ and in the homepage's #contact, once per page: a plain POST of I am a (`client_type`, radio chips: Homeowner (checked by default), Interior designer or architect, Business or hospitality, Other), The project (`project_scope`, radio chips, optional, none checked: One wall, A few rooms, Whole home, Office or venue), When (`timeline`, radio chips, optional, none checked: This month, In 1 to 3 months, In 3 to 6 months, No date yet; since 2026-11-01, Nov 1 report T1), Your name (`name`, required), Email (`email`, required), Tell me about your space (`message`, required), How did you find me? (`found_via`, radio chips, optional, none checked) with What did you ask it? (`ai_query`, shown and enabled only while an AI chip is checked) and Who referred you? (`referred_by`, a text field, optional, shown and enabled only while A friend or client or A designer or architect is checked, `REFERRAL_VIA`, and hidden and disabled again after a reset and on pageshow, so hidden text is never posted; since 2026-11-01, Nov 1 report T1) (since 2026-10-06, mockup A: the select became chips whose labels carry the twins, the radios are visually hidden but keyboard focusable, the value stays English; the phone field was dropped then, and since 2026-11-18 (Ron) Phone (`phone`, `type="tel"`, optional, the visitor's own number) sits after Email, half width on its own row below Your name and Email; Formspree mails it with the rest, `generate_lead` never carries it), The budget (`budget`, radio chips, optional, none checked: "I have a number in mind", "Flexible, for the right work", "I would like help planning it"; since 2026-11-28, the last field before Send, after How did you find me? and its two follow-up fields; no figure in any chip), the hidden `_subject` (New inquiry from stavtheodor.com), `artist` and `source_post` (since 2026-11-18, hidden and disabled in the markup; `form_js()` enables and fills them only on /contact/ reached from an Art Radar inquiry link, a `data-ask` link from `content/radar.json` `posts.<slug>.ask` or a hub artist's `ask`, and then also prefills the message while the box is empty and names the artist and the post in the subject; GA4 gets `radar_inquiry` on the click and `generate_lead` gains `lead_context` and `post_slug`, never a name, email or message), `page` (the page's path, in the markup), `landing_page` and `referrer` (since 2026-11-15, set by `form_js()`: the path of the visit's first page in this tab, and the host and path of the site that sent the reader there, never a query string; kept in sessionStorage for the visit, empty without JavaScript) and the `_gotcha` honeypot; the button says Send, with no note under it: the form asks for no photos, plans or files, and nothing next to it asks for one by email (the site owner, 2026-11-28; this replaced the earlier note asking for the wall photo by email). Since 2026-10-06 (mockup A, Ron) `contact_act()` renders the form inside one card (`.act-card`): the Contact eyebrow (dropped on /contact/, whose header says it), Tell me about your space. and three check lines (`CONTACT_HEAD`, `CONTACT_LINES`), then Or reach me directly (the address and the phone) beside the form; the homepage's #contact no longer repeats the closing line above it. The What happens next steps (`NEXT_STEPS`) render only in the no-form block (FORM_ENDPOINT empty). No town field, no placeholder. Without JavaScript Formspree shows its own thank-you page; the enhancement (`form_js()`) posts FormData with fetch and `Accept: application/json` (a normal CORS request), and on `r.ok` resets the form, replaces it in place with the thank-you card (2026-09-29: `.contact-box` stacks the form and the card in one grid cell, so the box keeps its height; a CSS drawn check, "Thank you, {first name}." or "Thank you.", that Stav reads every message herself and replies from stav@stavtheodor.com, the spam folder line with a mailto link, and a While you wait row: projects, the Art Radar archive, Art Radar on WhatsApp; all twins, prerendered hidden in the markup; focus moves to the card's heading; no response time promise, none is in content/BRIEF.md) and sends the GA4 `generate_lead` event with no personal data; any other answer or a network error keeps the form and shows its bilingual error line with the email (mailto, so the fallback panel applies) and the phone. A second, separate form, Art Radar by email (`site_chrome.radar_subscribe()`, since 2026-11-18), takes one email address on Art Radar post pages, the /radar/ archive and the hub: its own `form.radar-sub` class (so `form_js()` never binds it and `generate_lead` never fires for it), hidden `_subject` (Art Radar by email: new subscriber), `form_type` (art-radar-monthly), `page` and `lang` (the language on screen at submit), the same endpoint and honeypot, and GA4 `radar_subscribe` (link_location, page_path, never the address). It renders only while `site_chrome.RADAR_SUBSCRIBE` is True. Never send a test submission from an AI session: Ron tests the live form himself. The form's Hebrew was drafted by Claude on 2026-09-29 for Ron and Stav to edit.

13. **Stav's fees are never stated, anywhere on the site (Ron, 2026-09-29, binding).** Not as dollars and not as percentages, and no percentage range for the industry either (the industry percentage range that sat on six growth pages, the six buyer pages, llms.txt and answers.md is gone). Since the value story (Ron, 2026-09-29, later the same day), cost copy never talks about fees at all: no fee models, no fee hand-off line, no commission as a payment, no percentages. Every answer about cost, galleries or auctions tells the value story instead: in most cases art costs less through a private curator than at a large auction or through a gallery (bidding pushes auction prices up; galleries price with big margins to cover the venue, inventory and gallerists; Stav has none of those costs), the price includes private, tailored consultation until the art is on the wall, and she represents the client, not a roster of artists. Keep "in most cases" and "in many cases", no numbers. The rule text is `content/BRIEF.md` section 5; `tools/check_pages.py` (`FEE`, over every string of a page) and `tools/check_variants.py` (`MONEY`) fail the build on a $ figure, a digit with %, or a number with "percent" (Hebrew too in the pages gate), with no "industry" exception any more. The ban covers what clients pay Stav; referral fees THEODORA pays partners (designers, brokers, advisors) may be stated (Ron, 2026-09-29). The for-designers referral FAQ stays.
14. **No Stav review step, the FACT-TRACE RULE, and no service boundary (Ron, 2026-09-30, binding).** No page, guide, FAQ or Hebrew twin waits for Stav's approval, sign-off or fact check; the gates are the SEO brief, the AIO pass, a fresh auditor, the Curator's proofread, `tools/check_pages.py`, `python3 build.py` ending "check_site: OK", and Ron's push. FACT-TRACE RULE: Anything stated as fact about how Stav works, what she does, where she has worked or what she has done must be traceable to content/BRIEF.md or to the answers Stav already gave in theodora-growth/stav-review/answers-2026-09-30/ and curator-record.md. Anything else is written as advice the reader can act on ('here is how to decide'), never as a claim about Stav or THEODORA. No invented clients, projects, credentials, years, prices or process steps. Service area: THEODORA is based in Tenafly, New Jersey and works online with clients anywhere; Westchester, Hoboken, California and any other city are in scope, and no page says she works only in one area. Google Business Profile and other listings keep the Tenafly base; an areaServed names target markets, never a limit (`content/BRIEF.md` section 1).
15. **Google review quotes (Ron, 2026-10-23).** A Google review may appear on the site only as an exact span of its text, attributed by first name and month, and only from reviewers Ron confirms are real clients: never family or anyone with a connection to the business, never a quote with a price or a staging line, no stars, no rating or review count, no Review or AggregateRating schema; the Hebrew view shows a translation labelled as translated. The quotes live in `site_chrome.REVIEWS` (`reviews_block()`, on the homepage above the #contact card and on /contact/ under the card). Canonical text: the private site rules, F8.
16. **Six widths, two languages, before it ships (Ron, 2026-11-15).** Every page a change touches is checked at 375, 700, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920 px wide, in English and in Hebrew, before the PR merges: no horizontal overflow over 2 px, no console error, every page answers 200, and both the first screen and the full page are captured at every width. A width that fails is fixed before the merge, never after. `node tools/check_render.js` still runs; it does not replace this check. Canonical text: the private site rules, V1.
17. **Iris SHIP on real screenshots for any visual change (Ron, 2026-10-25).** A change a visitor sees as layout, image, color, spacing or type (any diff to `templates/`, `css/`, `site_chrome.py` or an image counts) ships only after Iris, the site owner's art director agent, returns SHIP on the rule 16 screenshots. Iris judges the pixels, never the code or the builder's own report. FIX holds the change until its fix spec is done; REJECT drops it. Canonical text: the private site rules, V2.
18. **The `auto-ship` label is how reversible, validated changes ship (Ron, 2026-11-19).** A PR earns the label only with a PASS from a separate validator (never the session that built it), plus an Iris SHIP under rule 17 when the change is visual, after the rule 16 check. The label goes on one PR at a time, in ship order, and `ship.yml` merges it after `gate` (Section 1, rule 8). A one-way change (noindex, a redirect, deleting a page, a new fact, a wording the site owner chose, anything under `.github/`, DNS or the GA4 tag) never gets the label and waits for Ron's merge. Agents never run `gh pr merge`, never push to `main` and never force-push. One exception: Ron names the PR in the session itself; the agent may then try the merge once, and if anything blocks it, stops and hands the click back to Ron, never retrying in another form, tool or session. Unattended scheduled runs never run `gh pr merge`. Canonical text: the private site rules, P3 and P4.
19. **Art Radar posts from Stav's own system ship with no person's merge (Stav, with Ron present, 2026-11-27).** A PR from Stav's system that only adds or fixes Art Radar posts (content/posts.html, the post images in images/, content/radar.json entries for those posts, llms.txt and answers.md post lines, this file's Work Log, and the pages build.py regenerates from them) carries the `auto-ship` label when it is opened, and `ship.yml` merges it once `gate` is green; `gate` is the check for these (the build, the dash, phone, twin, link and language gates). No separate validator, no Iris step and no Ron merge for these (Ron approved in Stav's session, 2026-11-27): the post images are Stav's own content, not a layout, image or design change of the site. Anything else in the same PR (templates, css, site_chrome.py, tools, a page that is not a post) puts it back under rules 17 and 18. The Hebrew is Stav's published text verbatim; the English is a full translation.

## 4. Standard workflow for adding or editing a post

Full templates and step-by-step detail: `ADD-BLOG-POST-GUIDE.md` (same directory).

Inbound growth work plan and Grok Bot coordination: content/INBOUND-PLAN.md (read before inbound/SEO/CTA work; log status there).

November 1 page-by-page report and R tasks: content/reports/2026-11-01/CLAUDE-TASKS.md (R1 to R8, after the open T tasks).

Short version:

```
1. git fetch origin && git status        # sync check (Section 1)
2. Edit content/posts.html: JSON-LD <script> + <article> pair at the TOP, right under
   the POST TEMPLATE comment, with BOTH language blocks (Hebrew + full English).
   NEVER put a post in index.html: it is generated, and the build stops if it finds one there.
3. Add image(s) to images/; add the slug to its topics in content/radar.json (or to no_topic)
   Every event in the JSON-LD "about" with no startDate: its opening date from the VENUE'S OWN page under
   posts.<slug>.events in content/radar.json (start_date, start_date_source; null if the page states none),
   never in Stav's text, never a guess (Ron, 2026-11-20, ron-decisions row 22; ADD-BLOG-POST-GUIDE.md Step 2.2)
   Every new post gets a topic and a bridge (posts.<slug>.bridge in content/radar.json: a target page picked for
   search fit, and line_en and line_he written in Stav's first person, outside her post text) in the same PR or the
   next one. A late post (dated 14 or more days before it goes up) gets a bridge to a page no other post bridges to.
4. python3 build.py                      # regenerates radar/<slug>/, radar/, index.html (its
                                         # timeline shows the six newest posts), sitemap.xml,
                                         # then runs the gates (dashes, phones, twins, links, lang...)
   node tools/check_render.js            # the language default and the switch, in a real browser
5. Update the llms.txt and answers.md post lists (real /radar/<slug>/ URLs); agent.txt has no post list
6. Append a line to the Work Log in AGENTS.md
7. git add <your files only> && git commit && git push origin <your branch>, then open a PR into main (Section 1, rule 8)
8. Verify live (allow ~10 min CDN cache; bypass trick in Section 2)
9. python3 tools/indexnow.py --since <the commit before yours>   # waits for the changed pages to
                                         # answer 200 live, then pings IndexNow (Bing, ChatGPT search,
                                         # Perplexity) in one batch with the key file at the root
```

---

## 5. Never do

- Never hand-edit or revert a sitemap `<lastmod>` or `tools/sitemap-lastmod.json` (2026-10-01, replacing the 2026-09-30 second-pass rule). `tools/build_sitemap.py` fingerprints each built page (its text in both languages, link, image, alt and meta values, and JSON-LD; the shared nav and footer, other scripts, style blocks, HTML comments and `?v=` cache busters left out) and keeps a page's lastmod until that fingerprint changes; a changed page is dated at the build. Commit `tools/sitemap-lastmod.json` and the sitemaps together with the content commit: no second pass is needed, and a rebuild of an unchanged tree changes nothing. On a merge conflict in `tools/sitemap-lastmod.json`, take either side and rebuild (or delete the conflicting lines: a URL missing from the file is dated from git, by the newest commit that changed its fingerprint). A chrome-only change (nav, footer, `PAGE_JS`, a code comment, `css/theme.css`) therefore moves no lastmod, by design.
- Never hand-edit a generated file: `index.html`, `radar/` (posts and the archive), `advisory/`, `projects/`, `for-*/`, `guide/`, `art-curator-new-jersey/`, `art-curator-new-york/`, `art-consultant-tenafly-nj/` and `art-advisor-bergen-county/` (since 2026-09-29), `art-advisor-westchester-ny/` (since 2026-09-30), the redirect stubs (`2/`, `questions/`, `services/`, `portfolio/`, `blog/` and `radar/what-to-see-july-2026/` since 2026-09-30, `press/` and `work-1/` since 2026-11-01 (old Wix addresses: `press/` and `work-1/tel-aviv-apartment/` noindex in `OLD_PATHS`, the Herzliya Pituach and St. Regis Amman project addresses merged into their project pages in `MERGED_PATHS`), `designers/` since 2026-09-29, `advisory/is-an-art-advisor-worth-it-for-one-piece/` (merged into the cost page's one or two pieces section since 2026-09-30, `MERGED_PATHS`), and `our-team/` and `our-team-1/`, which forward to /about/ since 2026-09-29, all written by `build-home.py`), `contact/` and `about/` (real pages since 2026-09-29, written by `build-site-pages.py`), the buyer variant folders (`/<path>/` for every `content/variants/*.json`, also written by `build-home.py`), `museum/artists/` (written by `museum/tools/build_artist_pages.py` from `museum/data/`, part of the build since 2026-09-29) and `sitemap.xml`. Edit `content/`, `templates/home.html`, `site_chrome.py` or `css/theme.css`, then run `python3 build.py`. The build overwrites every one of these files, and it refuses to run while a post `<article>` sits in `index.html`.
- Never add an Art Radar post anywhere but `content/posts.html`.
- Never force-push, never push to the deprecated `old-backup` remote.
- Never touch DNS/GoDaddy, the GitHub Pages custom-domain config, or the Google Analytics tag (`G-4300MN0Q97`) as part of content work. (The one approved change so far: the `navigator.webdriver` guard, Ron, 2026-09-30.) If the domain itself seems broken, stop and flag it to the site owner.
- Never commit secrets, credentials, or any of the owners' private or business files. This is a PUBLIC repo serving a public site.
- Never put internal notes, decisions, internal fee mechanics (referral fees that THEODORA pays partners may be stated on partner pages, Ron 2026-09-29), pending approvals or open questions for Stav or the site owner anywhere in this repo: the repo is public on GitHub, so everything under `content/` is readable there (the site stopped serving `content/` on 2026-10-01). They go to the site owner's private file, `the-system-v8-ron/B-brain/06-deliverables/theodora-growth/decisions-for-stav.md` in the private ron-brain repo. In a page JSON, an `editor_note` key or any key starting with `_` or `note` fails `tools/check_pages.py`, and with it the build (`content/PAGE-SPEC.md`).
- Never delete or commit another AI's untracked work-in-progress.
- Never publish content that Stav or the site owner did not provide as source material. Translations must trace to a Hebrew source post.

## 6. Verify before you claim done

```
curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" https://stavtheodor.com/            # 200
curl -s https://stavtheodor.com/radar/<slug>/ | grep -c 'post-body-en'    # >= 1
curl -s https://stavtheodor.com/radar/ | grep -c '<slug>'                 # >= 1 (the archive)
curl -s https://stavtheodor.com/ | grep -c '<slug>'                       # >= 1 if it is among the six newest
curl -s https://stavtheodor.com/sitemap.xml | grep '<slug>'               # present
```

Report failures honestly. If a check fails after the cache window, say so; do not declare success.

- 2026-11-28 (ET) | Claude (Theo builder, branch fix/hebrew-mirror-guides-radar) | Hebrew layout, CSS only, no wording changed (css/theme.css, ?v=20261128hr). - 2026-11-27 (ET) | Claude (branch first-load-2026-11-26, local, not pushed) | Wave 3, job 8 (Iris gate 3c, F3 blocker and F1): in Chromium on a touch screen, a page loaded already scrolled (a reload, or a return without the back/forward cache) showed the first room blended with the next, the wrong room, or an empty band once back at the top. Cause, traced with the opening's own state: ScrollTrigger measures its triggers during a refresh by scrolling to 0 and back, and css/theme.css sets html { scroll-behavior: smooth } (no Lenis on touch screens), so in Chromium that scroll to 0 is smooth and has not moved when the trigger is measured: the opening's start became minus the scroll offset (-232 after a reload at 232), its progress never reached 0 at the top, and the stage stayed at 0.32 opacity over a cross-faded canvas. WebKit scrolls at once and was never hit. Live has the same mismeasure (origin/main served locally: start -232 in 4 of 4 reloads), but there only the canvas shows it, as a partial cross-fade that a later refresh at the top can put right, so it showed intermittently; on this branch the phone stage's opacity follows that progress and stayed at 0.32 over the cross-fade, every time. js/home-opening.js (?v=20261126f): the refresh runs with scroll-behavior auto, read back at once (Chromium's scrollTo uses the style it last computed), and the smooth value returns when it ends; anchor links keep their smooth scroll. css/theme.css (?v=20261126t): on iPad-size portrait screens (768 to 820 px) with the phone opening the opening lines sit 32 px under the room, so a two-line caption never crowds the second headline (F1). Also (job 7 follow-up): once the after photo is on screen, the before <img> under it keeps the after photo as its fallback src too (THEODORA_PHONE.shown, called by js/home-opening-phone.js, ?v=20261126g), so if the <source> media stops matching both fall back to the dressed room on screen, never to the empty room.
- 2026-11-27 (ET) | Claude (branch first-load-2026-11-26, local, not pushed) | Wave 3, job 7: the phone stage drew the broken-image glyph when its <source> media stopped matching while the stage was still shown.
